# Gauntlet ledger

Every critic verdict, in the order it was given, with its evidence. Entries are appended by
`gauntlet/tools/record.mjs` from `gauntlet/verdicts/*.json` and never edited.

## 2026-10-02T22:39:48Z · p00-foundation · round 1 · BLOCKED

- Commit judged: `fe90a71ea0c41ba1db9e2e336bc8be590860242c`
- Critic: fresh critic, p00-foundation round 1
- Biggest gap: **The G1 HTTP attack misses a tenant leak through any parameter that is not an id** — The attack sends tenant B values in only a few places. Routes get B's GUIDs and its code. The query string gets fixed names (tenantId, tenant, id, userId, search, workspace). Body uuid fields get B's ids, and B's e-mail goes only to sign-in. Documented query parameters are ignored, and string fields such as email, code or name never carry B's real values. Nothing attacks the two reviewed SECURITY DEFINER lookups through new callers. So an endpoint that ignores the tenant by looking something up by e-mail, code or name passes the whole gate. For an ERP that will soon add contacts, products and documents looked up by code, TRN or e-mail, that blind spot is the main risk. Fix: drive every documented query, route and body parameter from the OpenAPI document with all of B's markers (ids, codes, e-mails, names, canary). Also fail when any endpoint other than auth.signIn and the session resolver calls identity.resolve_login/resolve_session. A related G2 blind spot: no gate checks that user create stays within the caller's grants.
- Gap evidence: In a clone of fe90a71, apply plants/plant-a2.diff. It adds GET /api/identity/users/lookup?email=, declares identity.users.read, and runs SELECT tenant_id, user_id FROM identity.resolve_login(@e) on the request's connection. Run `dotnet test tests/Erp.Gates.Tests -c Release`: 35/35 pass (plants/plant-a2.log). Add the test in plants/plant-proof.diff: signed in as tenant A admin, GET /api/identity/users/lookup?email=admin@<tenant B domain> returns tenant B's id (plants/plant-proof.log, test passes). The database confirms it: as erp_app, `SELECT * FROM identity.resolve_login('admin@gulfsteel.example')` returns gulfsteel's tenant_id, user_id and password hash (db-attack-app-role.txt, item 8). For G2, plants/plant-p2.diff removes the WithinCaller check on POST /api/identity/users; the suite passes 35/35 and the proof test mints an Administrator.
- Hard gates:
  - G1 tenant isolation (product as built): **PASS** — Gate suite passes (G1 DB plus HTTP: 25 endpoints, 2472+ requests). My own attack found no leak. Over HTTP I signed in as alnoor and sent 320 requests carrying gulfsteel ids in the route, tenant headers, query and body: 0 leaks, B rows untouched (attack/http_attack.out). As erp_app with no tenant set, every table returned 0 rows. Inserting or moving a row to B was refused by RLS. DISABLE RLS was refused (not owner). SET ROLE erp_auth/erp_owner was denied. UPDATE/DELETE on audit.entries was denied. CREATE in schemas was denied (db-attack-app-role.txt). No exports, jobs or file paths exist yet.
  - G1 tenant isolation (gate catches planted faults): **FAIL** — Planted 3 tenant leaks in a scratch clone (plants/*.diff, *.log). A1 (tenant_isolation policy on identity.roles changed to USING(true)) was caught by 2 DB tests. A3 (GET /api/tenancy/tenant honours ?tenantId=) was caught by the HTTP attack. A2 was MISSED: GET /api/identity/users/lookup?email= declares identity.users.read and calls the reviewed SECURITY DEFINER identity.resolve_login(). Gate suite: 35/35 passed (plants/plant-a2.log). plants/plant-proof.log shows the same endpoint returning tenant B's tenant_id and user_id to a tenant A admin.
  - G2 permissions (product as built): **PASS** — Every G2 test passes: fallback deny, anonymous allowlist, grant exactly one permission, no-roles user denied everything. In the browser, noaccess@ gets an empty menu and 'No access' at /identity/roles. viewer@ sees read-only screens.
  - G2 permissions (gate catches planted faults): **FAIL** — P1 (.AllowAnonymous() added to PUT /api/tenancy/tenant) was caught by 3 G2 tests and by G1. P2 was MISSED: I removed the RoleGrants.WithinCaller check from POST /api/identity/users and the gate suite still passed 35/35 (plants/plant-p2.log). plants/plant-proof.log shows a user holding only identity.users.create and identity.roles.read creating a user with the Administrator role, then signing in with every permission. No test anywhere covers granting roles beyond your own on user create.
  - G3 clean clone: **PASS** — `./erp verify` (project c-p00-foundation-r1, ports 20060/20061): 118 tests passed, rc=0, 86 s. `./erp up`: 100,004 users seeded and served on 20050, 25 s. `./erp verify --clean-clone` (G3 script: fresh clone, verify, up, HTTP sign-in): passed, 101 s. Ports and project overridable from the environment as documented. Timings are warm: the toolbox image, base images and the erp-cache-nuget/npm volumes already existed on this machine.
  - Rule gates (licence, float money, EN/AR string parity, audit coverage, OpenAPI, ratchet): **PASS** — All pass in the suite and the ratchet check passed. Manual check: API writes to tenants, roles, users and user_roles each left a field-level audit row with actor, tenant and correlation id (audit-sample.txt).
- Tests: 118 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped of 118 (`ERP_PROJECT=c-p00-foundation-r1 ERP_HTTP_PORT=20050 ERP_DB_PORT=20051 ERP_VERIFY_HTTP_PORT=20060 ERP_VERIFY_DB_PORT=20061 ./erp verify`, 86s)

- Other findings:
  - G2 gate blind spot (planted fault P2 missed): no test covers POST /api/identity/users assigning roles beyond the caller's own permissions. Users_cannot_escalate covers only role create/update and user update.
  - Scope gap: ModuleBuilder has no list/search registration hook, though p00-foundation.md lists 'list/search registrations' among what a module contributes from its own folder.
  - i18n has no plural rules. The users list shows '1 users'; the Arabic '{count} مستخدم' is wrong for 2 and for 3-10 (Arabic has six plural forms). A string framework every module will reuse needs ICU-style plurals.
  - The setup container prints 'Error: libgssapi_krb5.so.2: cannot open shared object file' on every `./erp up` and verify. It is harmless, but a newcomer sees an error line.
  - Sign-in failures increment lockout counters on every account with that e-mail in every tenant. Anyone who knows a tenant B address can pause B's sign-in for 15 minutes with 5 bad attempts. That is ordinary lockout behaviour, not a leak, but it has no per-account rate limit or CAPTCHA, and the per-IP limiter keys on RemoteIpAddress, which collapses behind a proxy.
  - The app role can set app.tenant_id at session level (non-local set_config works). The code uses transaction-local settings correctly; only the trust boundary is noted.
  - Demo tenant ids are predictable (0190a000-0000-7000-8000-00000000000N). Not a leak, but attack tooling and fixtures should not rely on it.
  - No compliance rules exist yet (docs/compliance absent), consistent with p00 having no statutory calculations. Money rounds the base amount half away from zero; once VAT or ledger rounding builds on it, that rule needs a source.
  - Browser walk: English and Arabic RTL from the first screen (ar-AE locale gives lang=ar dir=rtl). Sign-in, navigation, language switch and sign-out all work by keyboard, with focus kept in the form. Users search found 1 of 100,004 in 437 ms. No console errors. Back after sign-out shows the sign-in screen.
  - Tests at this commit: 97 .NET (42 kernel, 20 identity, 35 gates), 13 web unit, 8 Playwright e2e. All pass and none are skipped. Every ratchet minimum is met; there are no earlier verdicts to compare against.

## 2026-10-03T00:02:23Z · p00-foundation · round 2 · BLOCKED

- Commit judged: `c71d20e802db2b1a7a03db3076dc78bd5e7026a9`
- Critic: fresh critic, p00-foundation round 2
- Biggest gap: **The isolation gate guesses tenant-switch inputs instead of proving the tenant can only come from the session** — G1 HTTP sends tenant B's id in only six fixed header names (X-Tenant-Id, X-Tenant, Tenant-Id, X-Company-Id, X-Forwarded-Host, an erp_tenant cookie) and three fixed body names (tenantId, tenant_id, companyId). Body uuid fields otherwise get a random B id, so the tenant id lands there only by chance. Nothing checks how the tenant gets bound. Any code may call set_config('app.tenant_id', …) or IgnoreQueryFilters(), and nothing checks that the tenant each request's SQL runs under is the signed-in user's tenant. An endpoint that switches tenant on any header or field name not in those lists passes the whole gate, even though the spec says the tenant comes only from the session. Every later module (contacts, documents, imports, jobs) adds handlers that could do this.  Fix: (a) A source gate, like the existing one for reviewed functions: only ErpDbSession may name app.tenant_id or app.tenant_tx, and IgnoreQueryFilters/FromSql only in reviewed files. (b) A runtime check in the existing SqlTrace: inside an authenticated request, every statement after binding must run with current_tenant_id() equal to the principal's tenant claim. (c) A deterministic phase that sends B's tenant id in every uuid body field and in every X-* header seen in the app's source.  A related G2 blind spot: a POST/PUT/PATCH/DELETE endpoint may declare a *.read permission. A cheap rule (state-changing methods never declare an action named read) closes it.
- Gap evidence: In a clone of c71d20e, apply gauntlet/evidence/p00-foundation/r2/plant-A-hdr.diff. It is about 8 lines in UserEndpoints.List: when an X-Erp-Workspace header holds a uuid, it runs SELECT set_config('app.tenant_id', @t, true) on the request's connection and queries with IgnoreQueryFilters(). Run `dotnet test tests/Erp.Gates.Tests -c Release`: 45/45 pass, rc=0 (plant-A-hdr.log). Then add plant-A-hdr-proof-test.cs under tests/Erp.Gates.Tests/CriticProof/ and run it with --filter CriticProof. It passes: GET /api/identity/users?take=5 with X-Erp-Workspace: <tenant B id>, signed in as tenant A admin, returns tenant B users (viewer@bravo-cnry….example, 'Mariam Al Mansoori CNRY…' canary names; plant-A-hdr-proof.log). For G2: apply plant-P2.diff and the suite passes 45/45 (plant-P2.log). plant-P2-proof-test.cs shows a user holding only identity.users.read reactivating a deactivated user (204).
- Hard gates:
  - G1 tenant isolation (product as built): **PASS** — Gate suite passes. My own HTTP attack against the running demo (attack/http_attack.py, http_attack.out) signed in as alnoor admin (cookie and bearer), viewer, noaccess and anonymous. It sent 2,526 requests carrying gulfsteel ids in routes, 8 tenant-switch header variants including X-Erp-Workspace and Host, tenant query names, body tenantId/workspaceId fields, and every B e-mail, name and role name through search/q/email/code/name/filter. Result: 0 leaks, 0 server errors, B's checksums unchanged. Sign-in with workspace=gulfsteel kept the alnoor tenant. A B e-mail and an unknown e-mail get the same 401. As erp_app (db-attack-app-role.txt): 0 rows with no tenant set. With tenant A bound, B rows were invisible and inserting or moving rows into B was refused by RLS. A session-level app.tenant_id was ignored. DISABLE/NO FORCE RLS, SET ROLE owner/auth, row_security=off, UPDATE/DELETE on audit.entries, CREATE in schemas, COPY to file and pg_read_file were all denied. resolve_login returns nothing inside a bound transaction. No exports, jobs or file paths exist yet.
  - G1 tenant isolation (gate catches planted faults): **FAIL** — Planted 3 tenant leaks in a scratch copy. A-db (migration disables RLS on identity.user_roles) was caught: the migrator refuses to start, 45/45 tests fail (plant-A-db.log). A-body (POST /api/identity/users/query trusts workspaceId in the body) was caught, but only because random B ids in body uuid fields happened to include the tenant id (plant-A-body.log). A-hdr was MISSED: GET /api/identity/users honours an X-Erp-Workspace header by running set_config('app.tenant_id', header, true) and IgnoreQueryFilters(). Gate suite: 45/45 passed, rc=0 (plant-A-hdr.log, plant-A-hdr.diff). The proof test plant-A-hdr-proof-test.cs passes: a tenant A admin with that header gets tenant B's users, e-mails and canary names back (plant-A-hdr-proof.log).
  - G2 permissions (product as built): **PASS** — All 13 G2 tests pass: fallback deny, anonymous allowlist, grant exactly one, no-roles user denied, grant escalation. In the browser, noaccess@ gets 'No access' at /identity/roles (10-noaccess-direct-url.jpg). viewer@ sees only the Previous/Next buttons on Users.
  - G2 permissions (gate catches planted faults): **FAIL** — P1 (PermissionAuthorizationHandler succeeds for any signed-in user) was caught by 3 G2 tests (plant-P1.log). P3 (round 1's fault: WithinCaller removed on POST /api/identity/users) was caught by 2 tests, so the round 1 gap stays fixed (plant-P3.log). P2 was MISSED: POST /api/identity/users/{id}/reactivate is a write that reactivates and unlocks a user, and it declares only identity.users.read. Full gate suite: 45/45 passed (plant-P2.log, plant-P2.diff). The proof test plant-P2-proof-test.cs passes: a user holding only identity.users.read reactivates a user an administrator deactivated (204, isActive true; plant-P2-proof.log). Nothing stops a state-changing method from declaring a read permission.
  - G3 clean clone: **PASS** — `./erp verify` (project c-p00-foundation-r2, verify ports 20060/20061): 154 tests passed, rc=0, 150 s (verify.log). `./erp up` on 20050/20051 seeded 100,004 + 1,004 users and served in 31 s (up.log). `./erp verify --clean-clone` (fresh clone, verify, up, HTTP sign-in on 20080/20090) passed: rc=0, 195 s (g3-clean-clone.log). Timings are warm: the toolbox image and the NuGet/npm cache volumes already existed on this machine.
  - Rule gates (licence, float money, EN/AR strings with plurals, audit coverage, OpenAPI, ratchet): **PASS** — All pass in the suite, and the ratchet check passed (126 .NET / 19 web unit / 9 e2e against minimums of 126/19/9). By hand, API writes left field-level audit rows with actor, kind and correlation id (audit-sample.txt). Two ISC npm packages wait for owner approval in needs-human #3.

Ports and project name were overridden from the environment as documented. The setup container no longer prints the libgssapi error. Times are warm-cache.

The Odoo rig answers on :8069, but database listing is disabled and no sign-in has been published to critics, so I did not guess credentials. On a device that remembers the e-mail it is password then Enter: 15 keystrokes, about 5.6 s. Screenshots 01, 02, 08, 09. |

- Other findings:
  - The product's defence in depth works: with only the set_config plant (no IgnoreQueryFilters), the EF global filter still returned an empty list. The leak needs both bugs. Today no code under src/ reads a tenant from a request header or query, or calls IgnoreQueryFilters or FromSql.
  - Round 1 gaps stay fixed. A lookup endpoint calling resolve_login is now blocked by the unbound-only guard (0 rows inside a bound transaction), and by the source and trace gates. The round 1 create-user escalation plant (P3) is caught by 2 tests. ModuleBuilder.List exists. Plural messages are right in English and Arabic ('1 user', 'مستخدم واحد', '100,004 مستخدمين'). The libgssapi error line is gone from ./erp up.
  - Ratchet held. Suite 126 .NET, 19 web unit and 9 e2e meet the committed minimums, and every count rose from round 1's 97/13/8. The ratchet file was not loosened.
  - The sign-in lockout counter still increments on every account with the same e-mail in every tenant. Knowing a tenant B address lets anyone pause B's sign-in for 15 minutes with 5 bad passwords. This is not a data leak, but there is no per-account throttle or CAPTCHA. The Arabic and English error text also states the policy (5 attempts, 15 minutes).
  - The app role can call identity.resolve_login(email) on an unbound connection and get any tenant's tenant_id, user_id and password hash (db-attack-app-role.txt). This is the reviewed sign-in path, and the HTTP surface does not expose it. It does mean any SQL injection in the app reads every tenant's credential hashes.
  - Users search uses ILIKE '%term%' on email_normalized and display_name. There is no trigram index, only a btree on (tenant_id, display_name). Searches took 196-350 ms over 100,004 users here, which is fine now. It will not hold for p05/p16 at 100k+ rows with joins.
  - The Arabic screens show Latin digits (100,004; 2026/10/02). That is acceptable for the UAE, but there is no per-user numeral choice yet.
  - Switching language in the shell flips to RTL in 24 ms and saves to the profile (PUT /me/preferences 200, audited). If the page reloads before that request returns, the old language comes back from the session. This is minor.
  - Money has a value type (decimal amount, currency, rate, base amount; numeric(19,4)/(19,8)), but no table stores money yet, and the type does not carry the base currency's code.
  - A fresh visit logs a 401 from the session probe as a console error. It is cosmetic.
  - The Odoo rig is up on :8069, but gauntlet/compare/ is missing at this commit and no credentials are published. No task could be compared blind.

## 2026-10-03T00:18:29Z · p01-odoo-rig · round 1 · BLOCKED

- Commit judged: `21025788df0ae77f4fb2f1b6d27e2122cba15e6a`
- Critic: fresh critic, p01-odoo-rig round 1
- Biggest gap: **The G1 gate misses a cross-tenant leak through shared process state, such as a static or singleton cache** — The G1 HTTP attack only ever signs in tenant A's users. Tenant B never sends a request to the app under test, so B's data never enters process-wide state: static fields, singletons, IMemoryCache, output caching, or anything keyed without the tenant. In production both tenants share one process, so this whole class of leak can ship. I added a 3-line static cache to GET /api/tenancy/tenant. The gate suite (45/45) and the full .NET suite (126/126) still pass, while tenant A's admin receives tenant B's tenant record. The gate needs B (and A) to be active at the same time: drive B's users through every GET and write first, interleave them with A's attack, then look for B's markers in A's responses and the reverse. A self-test should plant a static cache to prove the gate catches it.
- Gap evidence: Reproduce: `git apply gauntlet/evidence/p01-odoo-rig/r1/plants/plant-a4-static-cache.diff && dotnet test tests/Erp.Gates.Tests -c Release`, which reports 'Passed! Failed: 0, Passed: 45' (plants/plant-a4-static-cache.log). The full `dotnet test Erp.slnx -c Release` also passes (plants/plant-a4-full-dotnet-suite.log). Proof of the leak: apply plants/plant-a4-proof.diff (it adds SelfTests/CriticPlantProof.cs) and run with --filter CriticPlantProof. The test signs in gulfsteel's admin, then alnoor's admin, and asserts alnoor's GET /api/tenancy/tenant contains gulfsteel's tenant id. It passes (plants/plant-a4-proof.log). Script: plants/run-plants.sh.
- Hard gates:
  - G1 tenant isolation (product as built): **PASS** — Gate suite passes. I attacked as alnoor admin against gulfsteel over HTTP: 209 requests sending B's user and role ids, tenant id, code and e-mails in routes, query strings and tenant headers, plus PUT/DELETE on B's users and roles and POST user with B's role ids. Result: 0 leaks. Writes to B returned 404 or 400 (attack/http_attack.out). As erp_app with no tenant set: 0 rows. Bound to A: only A's 100,004 users visible. UPDATE on B's rows changed 0 rows. INSERT into B was refused by RLS. DISABLE RLS was refused (not owner). SET ROLE erp_owner was denied (db-attack-app-role.txt). p01 changes no product code (git diff c71d20e..HEAD touches only build/, the licence gate test and the harness/rig). No exports, jobs or file paths exist yet.
  - G1 tenant isolation (gate catches planted faults): **FAIL** — Plant A4 was MISSED. It adds a process-wide static cache to GET /api/tenancy/tenant (plants/plant-a4-static-cache.diff). The gate suite still passed 45/45 (plants/plant-a4-static-cache.log), and so did the whole .NET suite, 126/126 (plants/plant-a4-full-dotnet-suite.log). A proof test confirms the leak: tenant B's admin calls the endpoint, then tenant A's admin calls it and receives tenant B's id (plants/plant-a4-proof.diff and .log, passed). Two other plants were caught. A2 replays the earlier round's resolve_login lookup endpoint and failed 3 tests: the source gate, the HTTP attack and a self-test. A5 disables and un-forces RLS on identity.user_roles, and the host refused to start with 'Security invariants violated'.
  - G2 permissions (product as built): **PASS** — Every G2 test passes in ./erp verify: fallback deny, exactly one permission or reviewed anonymous, no-roles user denied everything, grant escalation on create and update, and list permission checks. The e2e test 'a user whose roles grant nothing sees no menu' passes.
  - G2 permissions (gate catches planted faults): **PASS** — P2 replays the earlier round's plant, which removes RoleGrants.WithinCaller from POST /api/identity/users. It was caught by 3 tests (Creating_a_user_cannot_grant_more_than_the_caller_holds, No_endpoint_grants_more_than_the_caller_holds and a self-test), so the round 1 gap stays fixed. P3 adds POST /api/identity/users/{id}/deactivate with RequireAuthorization() only. The host refused to start ('declares no permission') and all 45 gate tests failed (plants/plant-p3-no-permission.log).
  - G3 clean clone: **PASS** — `./erp verify` from my clone (ERP_PROJECT=c-p01-odoo-rig-r1, ports 20150/20151/20160/20161) returned rc=0 in 183 s with 199 tests passed and the ratchet passed (verify.log). `./erp up` returned rc=0 in 26 s and served the seeded demo on 20150 (up.log). The Odoo rig also built from scratch in my own project (ODOO_REF_PROJECT=c-p01-odoo-rig-r1-odoo, ODOO_REF_PORT=20152): rc=0 in 161 s with every main list at 100,000 or more. A second run took 11 s and added 0 rows, so it is idempotent (rig-up-first.log, rig-up-second.log). Ports and project names are overridable from the environment as documented. Timings are warm: the images and NuGet/npm caches already existed.
  - Rule gates (licence, float money, EN/AR strings, audit, OpenAPI, ratchet): **PASS** — All pass in ./erp verify. The ratchet check passed, and no count fell below gauntlet/ratchet.json (suite.dotnetTests 126, webUnitTests 19, e2eTests 9, compareTests 45). The harness dependency (playwright-core) is Apache-2.0 and pinned.

## 2026-10-03T02:03:56Z · p01-odoo-rig · round 2 · BLOCKED

- Commit judged: `5a3c97fbc240c686c1991fa814322d4654f43016`
- Critic: fresh critic, p01-odoo-rig round 2
- Biggest gap: **The G1 gate misses a cross-tenant leak through closure-captured state that is returned in a response header** — The round-1 static-cache gap is now covered by a reflection inventory and by two-tenant concurrent activity. Each of those checks still has a blind spot, and together they let a real leak ship. (1) ProcessState.InspectTypes skips every compiler-generated type (IsGenerated), so state captured by an endpoint lambda (a `<>c__DisplayClass`) is never inventoried. Minimal-API handlers written as lambdas are the most common place for that state. (2) The HTTP judges (TenantActivity.SendAsync and IsolationAttack.Judge) search only the response body and the Location header for the other tenant's markers. Every other response header (ETag, Content-Disposition for exports, custom headers, Set-Cookie, Link) is never examined. With both, tenant A's admin receives tenant B's id, code and name, and the whole gate suite stays green. Fix both: inventory fields of closure display classes reachable from endpoint delegates (or forbid captured mutable locals in endpoint mapping), judge every response header in both directions, and add self-tests for a closure cache and a header leak.
- Gap evidence: In a clone at 5a3c97f, apply gauntlet/evidence/p01-odoo-rig/r2/plants/plant-B-closure-header.diff (or copy plants/B-closure-header.cs over src/Modules/Tenancy/Erp.Modules.Tenancy/TenancyEndpoints.cs). Then run `dotnet test tests/Erp.Gates.Tests -c Release`: rc=0, every gate test passes (plants/plant-B-closure-header.log). Proof: copy plants/CriticPlantProof.cs into tests/Erp.Gates.Tests/SelfTests/ and run with `--filter CriticPlantProof`. The test passes and prints 'tenant A received header X-Previous-Workspace: 0190a000-0000-7000-8000-00000000000b bravocnryea73bbffc3 Bravo CNRYEA73BBFFC3 LLC' (plants/plant-B-proof.log). Control: the same closure returning B's record in the body (plants/plant-A-closure-cache-body.diff) is caught with 214 leaks, so the miss is the header channel plus the uninventoried closure. Script: plants/run-plants.sh.
- Hard gates:
  - G1 tenant isolation (product as built): **PASS** — Gate suite passes in ./erp verify. My HTTP attack ran as the alnoor admin while the gulfsteel admin was active and warmed every GET. It sent 163 requests with B's user and role ids, tenant id, code and e-mails in routes, queries and six tenant-switch header and cookie variants. It also tried PUT and DELETE on B's users and roles and POST user with B's role ids. Result: 0 leaks in bodies or headers. Writes returned 404 or 400, and B's tenant was unchanged (attack/http_attack.out). As erp_app (db-attack-app-role.txt): unbound, 0 rows. With the GUC set but no transaction marker, 0 rows. Bound to A, only A's 100,004 users and A's audit and roles rows. UPDATE on B changed 0 rows. INSERT into B was refused by RLS. DISABLE RLS was refused (not owner). SET ROLE erp_owner was denied. The only SECURITY DEFINER functions are resolve_login and resolve_session (reviewed). No exports, jobs or file paths exist yet.

In TenancyEndpoints.Map, a lambda-captured `string? previousCaller` (a closure, not a static field or singleton) stores the last caller's workspace. GET /api/tenancy/tenant returns it in a response header, X-Previous-Workspace (plants/plant-B-closure-header.diff). The gate suite still passed: rc=0, 105 s (plants/plant-B-closure-header.log). A proof test passed: tenant A's admin received 'X-Previous-Workspace: 0190a000-0000-7000-8000-00000000000b bravocnryea73bbffc3 Bravo CNRYEA73BBFFC3 LLC' (plants/CriticPlantProof.cs, plants/plant-B-proof.log). There are two blind spots. ProcessState.IsGenerated skips compiler-generated closure classes. TenantActivity.SendAsync and G1HttpIsolationTests judge only the body and the Location header, never other response headers. Three other plants were caught. Plant A (the same closure cache returning B's DTO in the body) failed G1HttpIsolation with 214 leaks and the gate self-test. Plant C1 (roles policy `OR current_tenant_id() IS NULL`) failed 3 G1 database tests. The round-1 plant (a static cache) is now covered by the gate self-test LeakyModule bug 9.
  - G2 permissions (product as built): **PASS** — Every G2 test passed in ./erp verify: exactly-one-permission matrix, no-roles user denied everything and sees no menu, grant escalation, fallback deny, anonymous 401s. The e2e 'a user whose roles grant nothing sees no menu' passed.

It was caught by Every_declared_permission_is_in_the_catalogue_and_every_catalogue_permission_is_used (plants/plant-D2-write-guarded-by-read.log). Only that test caught it, and only because tenant.update became unused. The matrix test trusts each endpoint's declared permission, so a write guarded by a read permission that is used elsewhere would pass.

- Other findings:
  - Instrument bug (machine seconds): runner.execute calls op.finish() and then op.shot('done'). Operator.shot then moves t0 forward by the screenshot's duration while t1 stays fixed. So every result's machine_seconds is understated by the done screenshot's cost. Measured on my rig: switch-company was -0.099 s against a 0.147 s timeline, add-rate 1.353 s against 2.922 s, configure-sequence 1.645 s against 2.731 s, and find-record lost 0.178 s. The error depends on the page, so it is biased: Odoo lost 0.17-0.20 s and ours 0.05-0.07 s on sign-in. All 18 committed baselines are affected. In sign-in and switch-company the system wait exceeds machine seconds, which is impossible (machine-seconds-check.txt, committed-baselines-machine-check.txt). baselines.test does not check machine_seconds >= system_wait_seconds or that it equals the last step or wait end.
  - Instrument gap (uncounted actions): drivers receive ctx.page inside run() and nothing stops them from acting on it directly. I changed the ours sign-in driver to type the password and press Enter with ctx.page.keyboard. The run was still 'verified', with steps 4->2, keys 57->36 and human seconds 19.06->13.18. All 67 harness unit tests still passed (plants/plant-H1-uncounted-driver-actions.diff and .log). Ours drivers will be written by later critics, so the harness should lint drivers for page or keyboard actions inside run() (and goto deep links), or hand run() a page proxy that counts or refuses them.
  - The G2 matrix test trusts each endpoint's declared permission. A write guarded by a read permission (plant D2) was caught only because tenant.update became unused. If the same permission still guarded another endpoint, the plant would pass.
  - Tasks: 18 are defined, but plan.md's p15 comparison ('do a screen task through the API') has no task.
  - Blindness: key.json is written into the same folder as shots/ and review.html. With --product both, ours always runs before Odoo, so file timestamps order the products. Both products' layouts remain recognisable, which is inherent and accepted.
  - Rig: the 'setup' step reports +1 on every run (cosmetic). The rig's own seeding is idempotent: data steps added +0 on the second run.
  - Fixed since round 1 and still fixed: create-restricted-user offers keyboard and pointer variants (best per metric). The switch-to-arabic tester works in Contacts. ERP_SEED_USERS_CSV loads the shared 100,000 users into our product, matching the rig's logins. Approver and buyer sign-ins can be overridden from the environment. 12 more tasks have Odoo drivers and baselines. The rig was rebuilt from empty, and every main list is at 100,000 or more (contacts 100,000; users 100,005; rates 100,000; audit messages 100,007; attachments 100,046; job runs 100,000; purchase orders 100,000 with 200 pending). en_US and ar_001 are active.

The UI is right to left in Arabic from sign-in (dir=rtl, lang=ar; screenshot 03) and English is left to right (screenshot 02). No Odoo code, views or text were found copied: drivers only operate its screens. The Odoo image (LGPL) runs as an external reference rig that the owner's bar requires; it is not a product dependency.

## 2026-10-03T05:14:05Z · p04-shell · round 1 · BLOCKED

- Commit judged: `893b42c05bc4257e430a7bd5ad06397bdee1d6d1`
- Critic: fresh critic, p04-shell round 1
- Biggest gap: **A cross-tenant leak through the piece's own preferences endpoint passes every gate and test** — The shell's only write endpoint is PUT /api/identity/me/preferences, which this piece extended with numerals. The gates do not protect it against process-shared state held in a lambda closure. G1ProcessStateTests skips compiler-generated types (IsGenerated, G1ProcessStateTests.cs:105/249), so a <>c__DisplayClass capture is never inventoried. G1's tenant B writes each endpoint once during warm-up, and TenantActivity.TouchAsync re-touches only GET endpoints right before tenant A attacks an endpoint (TenantActivity.cs:233-241), so B never writes /me/preferences just before A calls it. Any state that one tenant's write leaves and the next caller reads therefore goes unseen, whether it reaches the next caller through the body or a header. The same blind spot was named in p01 round 2 and is still open at this commit. Every later module that adds a write endpoint (approvals, numbering, imports) inherits it. Fix: inventory closure-captured fields of mapped endpoint delegates. Have tenant B call each write endpoint with a valid body immediately before every tenant A call of that endpoint, then judge A's body and every header for B's markers. Keep plants P1b and P1c as gate self-tests.
- Gap evidence: Plant (plant-P1c-closure-body-leak.diff): in ProfileEndpoints.Map, add `var previous = new SessionUser?[1];`, map the PUT through a lambda that passes `previous`, and return `current with { DisplayName = $"{current.DisplayName} (after {last.Email})" }` after `last = previous[0]; previous[0] = current;`. Result: Erp.Gates.Tests passed 53/53 and Erp.Modules.Identity.Tests passed 24/24 (plants/P1c/summary.txt). The header variant P1b gave the same result. On the running planted build (port 20485), B PUT {numerals:latn} followed by A PUT {numerals:latn} returned B's admin@gulfsteel.example to A (plant-P1-leak-demo.txt). The static-field version P3 was caught, so the miss is specific to closure-held state and write-then-read ordering.
- Hard gates:
  - G1 tenant isolation (suite and my own attacks on the product as built): **PASS** — The gate suite passed 53/53 inside ./erp verify. My HTTP attacks as alnoor (A) against gulfsteel (B) found no leak (attack-http.txt). I tried PUT /api/identity/me/preferences with B's userId, tenantId and id in the body; the X-Tenant-Id, X-Tenant, X-Workspace and X-User-Id headers on the session and preferences calls; query tenantId and workspace; GET and PUT /identity/users/{B id}; and a users search by B's e-mail. B's preferences were unchanged afterwards. As the database app role erp_app (attack-db-app-role.txt), an unbound read returned 0 rows and a read bound to A returned 0 rows of B. The UPDATE of B's row affected 0 rows. SET row_security=off was refused. erp_app and erp_owner have no BYPASSRLS. identity.users, roles, user_roles and sessions have RLS enabled and forced.
  - G1 catches a tenant leak planted in this piece's surface: **FAIL** — I planted the leak in PUT /api/identity/me/preferences, the preferences endpoint this piece extended. A closure-captured `previous` array in ProfileEndpoints.Map hands the previous caller's SessionUser to the next caller. The live demo of the planted build leaked: B saves a preference, then A's PUT returns admin@gulfsteel.example (plant-P1-leak-demo.txt). Variant P1b puts B's e-mail in a response header (X-Erp-Previous-Editor) and keeps the body correct. Variant P1c appends ' (after <B e-mail>)' to displayName in the body. Both passed Erp.Gates.Tests 53/53 and Erp.Modules.Identity.Tests 24/24 (plants/P1b, plants/P1c). A static-field version of the same leak (P3) was caught by G1ProcessStateTests and G1HttpIsolationTests.
  - G2 permissions: **PASS** — Plant P2 replaced RequirePermission(ProfileUpdate) with RequireAuthorization() on /me/preferences. ErpPlatform.ValidateEndpoints refused to start ('PUT /api/identity/me/preferences declares no permission'), so 53 gate and 24 identity tests failed. Plant P4 offered the palette's record sources without the permission filter. The web unit test AppShell.test.tsx caught it (63/64; the users API was called for a user without identity.users.read), and tsc also rejected that code. In the browser, no-access screens and the palette show only what the role grants.
  - G3 clean clone: one command builds, migrates, seeds and passes all tests: **FAIL** — `ERP_PROJECT=c-p04-shell-r1 ERP_HTTP_PORT=20450 ERP_DB_PORT=20451 ERP_VERIFY_HTTP_PORT=20460 ERP_VERIFY_DB_PORT=20461 ./erp verify` exited 1 after 647 s. shell.spec.ts:135 'Arabic-Indic digits are a per-user choice that follows the user' failed: input[name=email] was not found after sign-out because the user was still signed in (01-e2e-digits-test-still-signed-in.png, verify.clean.log). Rerun against a fresh stack, it failed 2 of 4 times. `./erp up` brought the 100,000-user demo up in 42 s.
  - Rule gates (licences, no float money, English/Arabic strings, audit, logical CSS, kernel formatter, accessibility): **PASS** — String check passed: 140 keys in English and Arabic, 4 plural messages. The accessibility check passed (239 JSX elements). The ShellGateTests and the licence, money and audit gates were in the 53 passing. ## 2026-10-03T06:48:15Z · p05-list-search · round 1 · BLOCKED

- Commit judged: `0ab81d7f7f9993b78f41ff6b71a0bd72337ff3d1`
- Critic: fresh critic, p05-list-search round 1
- Biggest gap: **The isolation gate cannot see a cross-tenant leak through the list engine unless a tenant B marker appears in the response** — Every list registered with p05 relies on G1 to catch leaks automatically, but G1 only flags a leak when one of tenant B's id or text markers shows up in a response. A shared cache in the list engine that carries tenant B's aggregates (totals, group counts, sums) into tenant A's answers passes every gate. The process-state gate also exempts the list bindings: ModuleCatalog._modules is reviewed wholesale, so instance fields of ListBinding<T> are never inspected. Grouping with totals is exactly where money modules will cache. The gate needs to (a) inspect ListBinding and other per-registration objects for mutable state, and (b) check list totals and group counts that tenant A receives against tenant A's own row count (for example, compare the first-page total with an exhaustive keyset walk, and the group counts with A-only database counts, after B ran the same queries). A second, smaller gate gap is G2: it never checks shared-view PUT/DELETE by a user who holds lists.views.share but not the list's permission.
- Gap evidence: Apply gauntlet/evidence/p05-list-search/r1/plants/L3-engine-count-cache.patch (a ConcurrentDictionary of totals keyed by search\|filter in ListBinding.QueryAsync). Run `dotnet test tests/Erp.Gates.Tests -c Release --filter FullyQualifiedName~Erp.Gates.Tests.G1`: everything passes (run-L3P1P2.log). Live on a stack with the plant: gulfsteel GET /api/identity/users?search=a → total 1004; then alnoor, same query → total 1004; alnoor with an uncached key → total 2005 (plant-live-demo.txt). For G2, apply P2-delete-without-list-permission.patch and run the G2 filter: 17/17 pass, while a lists.views.share-only user deletes the users list's Team view with 204.
- Hard gates:
  - G1 tenant isolation (product as built): **PASS** — The gate suite passes (G1HttpIsolation: 51 endpoints, about 34,000 requests). My own attacks as admin@gulfsteel against alnoor (100,004 users) all came back clean: search, filters (eq, in, an injected quote, % and _ wildcards), groupBy, X-Tenant headers, tenantId/tenant query parameters, a replayed and a forged keyset cursor, GET/PUT/DELETE on alnoor's shared and personal view ids. Database: erp_app has no BYPASSRLS. It sees 0 rows unbound and 0 alnoor rows when bound to gulfsteel, and an insert into lists.saved_views for the other tenant is refused by RLS. Details in attack-api.txt.
  - G1 catches faults planted in p05's surface: **FAIL** — Plant L3 (plants/L3-engine-count-cache.patch) adds a 60-second count cache to ListBinding.QueryAsync, keyed by search and filter with no tenant. G1HttpIsolationTests still PASSED (plants/run-L3P1P2.log, 5 min 3 s). On a live stack the leak is real: gulfsteel searches 'a' and gets total 1004; alnoor then searches 'a' and gets total 1004, while its true count is 2005 (plants/plant-live-demo.txt). G1ProcessStateTests also passed with page-cache plants L1, L2 and L3, because ModuleCatalog._modules is allow-listed wholesale and ListBinding instances are never inspected. L1 (page cache) and L2 (closure-captured views cache) were caught by the HTTP attack (run-L1L2.log, 1498 leaks).
  - G2 permissions (product as built): **PASS** — viewer: list 200, definition 200, personal view 201, create/delete shared view 403. noaccess: 403 on everything. Unauthenticated: 401. The gate suite passes.
  - G2 catches faults planted in p05's surface: **FAIL** — Plant P2 (plants/P2-delete-without-list-permission.patch) removes the list's own permission check from shared-view DELETE. All 17 G2 tests PASSED (plants/run-P2.log). Live: a user whose only role holds lists.views.share gets 403 on GET /api/lists/identity.users/views, but 204 on DELETE /api/lists/identity.users/shared-views/{id}, which deletes the workspace's Team view (plant-live-demo.txt). Plant P1 (sharing guarded by the read permission) was caught by Every_registered_list_is_readable_with_its_permission_alone.
  - G3 clean clone: **PASS** — Fresh clone at 0ab81d7. `ERP_PROJECT=c-p05-list-search-r1 ERP_HTTP_PORT=20550 ERP_DB_PORT=20551 ERP_VERIFY_HTTP_PORT=20560 ERP_VERIFY_DB_PORT=20561 ./erp verify` passed in 660 s and the ratchet check passed. `./erp up` with ERP_SEED_USERS_CSV (shared dataset) gave a running demo with 100,004 users in 58 s. Ports and project overrides worked.
  - Rule gates (licence, money, strings, audit, list index): **PASS** — Pass inside verify. Saved-view insert, update and delete all write audit.entries with the actor and old/new values (checked in the database).
- Tests: 433 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped of 433 (`ERP_PROJECT=c-p05-list-search-r1 ERP_HTTP_PORT=20550 ERP_DB_PORT=20551 ERP_VERIFY_HTTP_PORT=20560 ERP_VERIFY_DB_PORT=20561 ./erp verify`, 660s)
- Clean clone to running demo: PASS in 660s — `./erp verify` from a fresh clone took 660 s: 245 .NET, 89 web unit, 31 e2e and 68 compare tests, ratchet passed. `./erp up` gave a seeded demo in 58 s (verify.log, up.log).
- Odoo side by side:

  | Task | Ours steps / keys / s / human s | Odoo steps / keys / s / human s | Winner | Notes |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | find-user (find one user among 100,000; p16 contacts not built, so the critic used the largest list, users from the shared dataset) | 3 / 21 / 0.249 / 8.47 | 6 / 21 / 1.58 / 14.55 | odoo | Harness verdict: loss, because keystrokes tie at 21 (a tie is a loss). Ours wins steps, machine seconds and human seconds. Ours path: click Users > type the name > Enter, which opens the single result. Odoo's best per metric: Ctrl+K > /users > Enter > name > Enter > click, or Apps > Settings > Manage Users > name > Enter > click. Both products must type the full name plus Enter, so ours cannot win keystrokes until quick search ranks by relevance (for example 'yousef wang' + Enter opening the best match) or opens on fewer typed keys. The critic wrote the task and both drivers (compare-drivers/). Three runs per product, all verified. find-record (contacts) is not compared because p16 does not exist. |

- Other findings:
  - Keyboard defect: the column header buttons (sort 'Name', 'Options for the column E-mail' and the others) live inside the grid's onKeyDown. Enter on them opens the active record instead of sorting or opening the column menu, and Space does nothing. Sort and filter from headers is therefore mouse-only, against the keyboard-first rule (07-enter-on-column-options-opens-record.jpg; scripts/hdr.mjs: Tab to the button, press Enter, and the URL gains &open=<row id>).
  - Columns do not line up. With the default columns at 1440 px, rows with long e-mails shift the E-mail, Language, Status and Last sign-in cells right by up to 37 px, because each row's grid template sizes itself (01-users-list-en.jpg; email-cell x per row 543,543,543,543,551,566,543,...).
  - Bulk actions: the only built-in bulk action is Copy, and no screen registers a data-changing one. Ctrl+A selects only the loaded rows; there is no 'select all N that match' for acting on 100,000 rows, which Odoo offers.
  - Filters from headers are one condition per column, joined with AND. The engine supports or/not (checked through the API: '(language eq ar or isActive eq false) and not displayName startsWith M' → 29,504 in 41 ms), but the screens cannot build an OR.
  - Picking a record in the Ctrl+K palette only filters the users list (?q=email). A second Enter is needed to open it.
  - Arabic search does not normalise spelling variants: 'فاطمة' finds the user but 'فاطمه' does not; 'الزعابي' finds it but 'الزعابى' does not. For UAE data this is a chance to beat Odoo that is not taken.
  - Quick search ranks nothing: results keep the default sort (-createdAt). 'yousef wang' returns 46 rows with the wanted user anywhere. Relevance ranking (trigram similarity) plus Enter-opens-top-hit would cut keystrokes below Odoo's on find-one-among-100,000.
  - Performance is good. Over 100,004 users, round-trip medians are 8-42 ms for exact, partial, email and filter searches; 58 ms for search 'a' (all rows); 103 ms for 'Wa'; 182 ms for skip=99,000. Keyset walks over 7 sort orders (nullable lastSignInAt included) returned every row exactly once (api-timings.txt).
  - Positive: a list registers through ModuleBuilder.List with a typed filter language and its errors localised. Trigram GIN on display_name/email_normalized and (tenant_id, column, id) btree indexes exist. Saved views (personal/shared/default) persist across reloads, the viewer cannot share (no checkbox; API 403), and saved views are audited with actor and old/new values.
  - English and Arabic: the users list is fully RTL with Arabic chrome, plural counts are right ('100,005 مستخدمين'), and Enter-opens-single-result works in Arabic (08, 09). The only Latin text in the chrome is a user-named view.
  - [route: p03-identity] While testing the keyboard, three stray keys on the users screen (n, g, Enter) created a real user 'G' <g@alnoor.example> with no confirmation. The 'n' hotkey opens New user, and Enter submits a one-letter entry with an invented address. A guard or confirmation for a derived e-mail would prevent accidental accounts.
  - [route: p00-foundation] The G1 process-state gate allow-lists ModuleCatalog._modules wholesale ('list registrations with their query bindings… never tenant data'). Any mutable state hung off a module registration (list bindings, menus, future report or export registrations) is therefore invisible to the inspector. It should descend into registration objects rather than trusting the reason.
  - The comparison harness at this commit has no users-list task. The critic added tasks/find-user.mjs with ours and odoo drivers (kept in compare-drivers/). The Odoo keyboard path needs a wait for the palette input before typing '/users'.

## 2026-10-03T06:07:07Z · p03-identity · round 1 · BLOCKED

- Commit judged: `5a711de`
- Critic: fresh critic, p03-identity round 1
- Biggest gap: **The G1 gate misses a cross-tenant leak through this piece's own "what can this person do" endpoint** — A per-user-id cache on GET /api/identity/users/{id}/access, held in a local that the endpoint lambda captures, hands tenant B's roles and permissions to tenant A's admin. All 62 gate tests still pass. Two blind spots combine.  (1) The process-state inventory skips compiler-generated types, so closure state is never listed. This was reported in p01 r2 and is still open at this commit.  (2) The HTTP attack cannot see a per-id cache even when the leak is in the response body. TenantActivity.OwnPathAsync warms each route with one tenant B id per collection. IsolationAttack then attacks with up to 5 snapshot ids per table plus the tenant id, so the ids A attacks are seldom the ids B just warmed. With the static variant (T1), the HTTP attack also passed; only the reflection inventory caught it.  Fix: - Feed the exact route values tenant B used into tenant A's route attack, and have B touch every victim id before A attacks it. - List fields of closure display classes reachable from endpoint delegates, or forbid captured mutable locals in endpoint mapping. - Add self-tests for a closure cache and a per-id cache on a route with an id.
- Gap evidence: In a clone of 5a711de:  1. Apply gauntlet/evidence/p03-identity/r1/plants/plant-T2-closure-access-cache.diff. 2. Run `dotnet test tests/Erp.Gates.Tests -c Release`. It reports Passed 62 of 62 (plants/plant-T2.log). The identity, kernel and tenancy suites also pass (plant-T2-other-suites.log). 3. Copy plants/CriticPlantProof.cs into tests/Erp.Gates.Tests/CriticProof/ and run with --filter CriticPlantProof. Tenant B's admin reads its own access view, then tenant A's admin calls GET /api/identity/users/{B admin id}/access, gets 200, and receives B's Administrator role id and permission list (plants/plant-T2-proof.log).  The static variant plants/plant-T1-static-access-cache.diff passes G1HttpIsolation and fails only G1ProcessState (plants/plant-T1.log).
- Hard gates:

Search for B's e-mails returned nothing, and tenant headers and the workspace parameter were ignored (attacks/attack-a-vs-b.txt). As erp_app: 0 rows visible when no tenant is bound, password_hash denied, an insert claiming tenant B refused by RLS, and RLS cannot be disabled (attacks/db-app-role.txt).
  - G1 gate catches a tenant leak planted in this piece's surface: **FAIL** — Plant T2 adds a per-user-id cache, captured by a closure, to GET /api/identity/users/{id}/access, which is this piece's "what can this person do" endpoint. The whole gate suite still passes 62/62 (plants/plant-T2.log), and so do the identity, kernel and tenancy suites (plant-T2-other-suites.log). The proof test shows tenant A's admin getting 200 with tenant B admin's roles and permissions (plants/plant-T2-proof.log). The static-field variant T1 was caught only by the process-state inventory; the HTTP attack missed the leaked body (plants/plant-T1.log). Plant U, a global unique e-mail index, was caught, but only incidentally: G1SignInThrottle creates the same e-mail in two workspaces (plant-U-full-suite.log).

Escalation attempts are denied in the G2 suite. Plant P3, which removes the check on added roles in PUT /users/{id}, was caught by No_endpoint_grants_more_than_the_caller_holds (plants/plant-P.log).
  - G2 gate catches a permission fault planted in this piece's surface: **FAIL** — Plant P1 guards POST /users/{id}/unblock with identity.signIns.read, a read permission. Plant P2 drops the within-own-grants check on DELETE /roles/{id}. With both in place, G2 runs 15 of 16 passing (the one failure is P3's) and the identity tests pass 32/32 (plants/plant-P.log). The proof shows a user holding only signIns.read and users.read gets 204 from unblock, and a user holding only roles.delete and roles.read deletes a role granting users.create, users.update and resetPassword with 204 (plants/plant-P-proof.log). A UI plant that shows Delete role regardless of roles.delete passes 74/74 web unit tests, and no e2e spec opens Roles as such a user (plants/plant-UI.log).

- Other findings:
  - [route: p00-foundation] G2 trusts each endpoint's declared permission. A write guarded by a read permission passes every gate: plant P1 puts identity.signIns.read on POST /users/{id}/unblock. The seeded Read-only role holds signIns.read, so any read-only user could clear sign-in pauses (plants/plant-P-proof.log). This is the same class as p01 r2's D2 and is still open at this commit.
  - [route: p00-foundation] The HTTP attack checks for differential answers on GETs only. A global unique e-mail index (plant U) makes POST /api/identity/users answer 409 for tenant B's address and 201 for an unused one, which tells tenant A which addresses B uses (plants/plant-U-proof.txt). It was caught only because G1SignInThrottle happens to create the same e-mail in two workspaces. The database gate also never looks at unique indexes on tenant tables that leave out tenant_id.
  - [route: p00-foundation] Response headers other than Location are still not judged, so the p01 r2 header-leak gap is not fixed at this commit (G1HttpIsolationTests.cs passes only Location to Judge).
  - This piece's own G2 gaps: - The takeover and grant-escalation gates cover only /users/{id} and POST /roles/{id}/copy. DELETE /roles/{id} without its within-own-grants check (plant P2) passes, so a user with only roles.delete can delete roles granting permissions they lack. - The screens-hide-what-you-cannot-do check has no case for the Roles screen. Showing Delete role regardless of roles.delete passes 74/74 web unit tests, and no e2e spec opens Roles as a user who lacks roles.delete.
  - Scope gap: there is no per-user default company and no per-company role assignment. No company concept exists at this commit, and identity.user_roles has no company column. The piece spec requires both. For UAE groups running several LLC or free-zone entities, a role that cannot be limited to one company is the main thing a restricted role needs.
  - find-user loses on a keystroke tie, 24 against 24. Search is one contiguous ILIKE string. Word-order-independent multi-word search ("rania kaabi" is 11 keys), or focusing the search box when Users opens, would beat Odoo outright.
  - create-restricted-user: no module permission beyond identity and workspace exists, so the task's "contacts only" role cannot be built yet. The win was measured with the Read-only role.
  - Users list: - No sort, no filter by role, status or inactive, and no export. - Offset paging only. - Search is a full ILIKE scan taking 0.21 to 0.25 s at 100,004 rows, with no trigram index (users-search-timings.txt). - The list is hand-built, not the shared list framework.
  - Users cannot be deleted, and the e-mail cannot be changed after creation. A typo in an invitation leaves an orphan account. Odoo lets the login be edited. The ours driver had to remove its test user through the database.
  - Arabic layout defects: - The sign-in history table overflows the user panel, so the Session column is cut off, and the paused-until line has broken bidi order (screenshot 07). - The inactive status cell is clipped at the list edge (screenshot 04). - The matrix "Change" header is clipped in English (screenshot 03). - Users have one display name with no Arabic name, so the Arabic list shows Latin names even though the shared dataset carries name_ar.
  - Lockout works per client. After 6 failures the right password is refused from that client, and the admin sees the pause with "allow sign-in again" (lockout-and-history.txt). Behind Docker every client arrives as 172.19.0.1, and compose sets no KnownProxies. A deployment that misses the README setting therefore lets one attacker pause an account for the whole office.
  - The seeded Read-only role includes identity.signIns.read, so every read-only user sees all colleagues' IP addresses and browsers. identity.sign_in_attempts has no retention. These are personal data under UAE PDPL; worth an owner decision.
  - identity.verify_sign_in takes the throttle source as a parameter from the app role. With any SQL injection, the per-client throttle could be bypassed by rotating p_source. An address that exists in two workspaces costs two PBKDF2 derivations, so timing shows it is used in more than one workspace.
  - Positive: - Tenant isolation held in every API and database attack. - Audit covers users, roles, user_roles, credentials (redacted) and sessions. - Arabic is right to left from sign-in (dir=rtl, lang=ar), with no Latin words on the Arabic roles screen. - No console errors or 5xx during the walk. - Test counts rose from p00 r2's 126/19/9 to 158/74/24 plus 68 compare tests, and the ratchet holds.
  - Harness note: verify's Testcontainers bind random host ports outside the critic's assigned block, which cannot be overridden. Comparing find-user needs ERP_SEED_USERS_CSV. The added find-user task and drivers are in compare-drivers/ for the recorder to merge.
- Screenshots: [01-effective-permissions-en.jpg](evidence/p03-identity/r1/01-effective-permissions-en.jpg), [02-user-details-en.jpg](evidence/p03-identity/r1/02-user-details-en.jpg), [03-roles-matrix-en.jpg](evidence/p03-identity/r1/03-roles-matrix-en.jpg), [04-users-ar-rtl.jpg](evidence/p03-identity/r1/04-users-ar-rtl.jpg), [05-roles-matrix-ar-rtl.jpg](evidence/p03-identity/r1/05-roles-matrix-ar-rtl.jpg), [06-roles-read-only-user.jpg](evidence/p03-identity/r1/06-roles-read-only-user.jpg), [07-sign-in-history-paused-ar.jpg](evidence/p03-identity/r1/07-sign-in-history-paused-ar.jpg), [a76e9c20c473ae04.jpg](evidence/p03-identity/r1/compare/shots/a76e9c20c473ae04.jpg), [44e586fd0d9c0057.jpg](evidence/p03-identity/r1/compare/shots/44e586fd0d9c0057.jpg), [aaa9e6afbde8aa7c.jpg](evidence/p03-identity/r1/compare/shots/aaa9e6afbde8aa7c.jpg), [472fc798e782933f.jpg](evidence/p03-identity/r1/compare/shots/472fc798e782933f.jpg)

## 2026-10-03T07:21:51Z · p01-odoo-rig · round 3 · BLOCKED

- Commit judged: `b0981bddc40abae4d9d708200c8929707969408b`
- Critic: fresh critic, p01-odoo-rig round 3
- Biggest gap: **The instrument trusts everything a driver does outside the clock, and several things it declares inside it, so an ours driver can win without doing the task as a user would** — The round-3 guard closes actions taken during the measured part through the page, keyboard, network and waitFor. It does not close these: (1) Page or context scripts installed in setup or signIn (page.evaluate, addInitScript, exposeFunction) are allowed and keep acting after the clock starts. The sentinel only covers the waitFor condition. (2) The start state is not checked. signIn may end on any screen with any input already typed, although the README promises 'the screen the product shows right after sign-in'. (3) op.shot() inside run() is subtracted from machine seconds while the product keeps working, so screenshots hide latency. (4) op.type accepts '\n', which presses Enter without counting a step. (5) `chain` (no M operator) is the driver's own claim and is never checked. Later critics write the ours drivers, so each of these lets a gamed or careless driver turn a loss into a recorded win. Fixes: snapshot and verify the start state (URL plus focused element plus empty inputs, or a product-neutral start check per task). Forbid evaluate, addInitScript, exposeFunction and route in drivers (lint plus guard), or reload the page before start() so set-up scripts are gone. Stop excluding screenshot time inside run(), or forbid op.shot while a step's response is pending. Reject control characters in op.type. Derive chain from the step sequence (Enter after type, type after a click on the same field) instead of trusting the flag. Add a plant test for each.

## 2026-10-03T17:34:18Z · p04-shell · round 2 · BLOCKED

- Commit judged: `01fd70b02bbe759d41885c254cb7d498ddbdd4a1`
- Critic: fresh critic, p04-shell round 2
- Biggest gap: **The shell's client state is outside every isolation gate: a palette cache that survives sign-out shows tenant B's users to tenant A, and every gate passes** — The G1 machinery covers only the server: routes, database, process state and headers. The shell is a single-page app that stays in memory across sign-out and the next sign-in, and no gate or test ever signs in as two tenants in one browser. So any module-level cache, store or memoised answer in the shell or a palette source leaks the previous tenant's records to the next user of the same tab. A UAE trading company's outsourced bookkeeper, serving several client companies (tenants) from one browser, is exactly that user. The real product shows a milder form of the same thing today: after tenant B signs out, the sign-in page is pre-filled with B's e-mail (erp.lastEmail in localStorage, SignInPage.tsx:6-14). Fix: (1) Make sign-out and every tenant change reload the document (location.assign) and clear the erp.* keys that belong to the user, so JS memory cannot carry over. (2) Add an end-to-end gate: in one tab, tenant B uses every palette source, list, screen and dialog with marker data, then signs out; tenant A signs in in the same tab, repeats the same actions, and the DOM, localStorage, sessionStorage and IndexedDB are judged for B's markers. (3) Keep plant P9 as the gate's self-test.
- Gap evidence: Plant (plant-P9-palette-cache-survives-sign-out.diff): in CommandPalette.tsx, add `const answered = new Map<string, SourceState>()` at module level. Serve a hit for `${source.key}:${trimmed}` instead of asking the source, and store each answer. Results: tsc, the string and accessibility checks and the unit tests (93/93) passed. The end-to-end suite against the planted build passed 34/34 on its second run (plants/P9/e2e-2/e2e.log); the first run's one failure was an unrelated preferences-save flake. The .NET gates do not touch client code. ## 2026-10-03T17:12:03Z · p00-foundation · round 3 · BLOCKED

- Commit judged: `62bd546`
- Critic: fresh critic, p00-foundation round 3
- Biggest gap: **The one command fails from a clean clone under load: bulk credential seeding hits Npgsql's 30 s default command timeout** — At 62bd546, `./erp verify` from a fresh clone failed twice in a row on this shared 4-CPU machine. Erp.Modules.Identity.Tests.UsersVolumeFixture seeds 100,000 users, then copies their credentials in one INSERT INTO identity.user_credentials ... SELECT ... FROM identity.users (IdentitySeeder.SeedVolumeAsync, line ~126-134). That statement has no CommandTimeout, so Npgsql's 30 s default applies, and the per-row audit trigger and RLS make it slow under load. The fixture throws and all 3 UsersListVolumeTests fail. Because the suite fails, the end-to-end stage and the ratchet check never run. ./erp up uses the same statement and failed the same way on an earlier attempt of this code (rc=1 after 122 s). Run alone at a lower load, the tests pass in 85 s, so G3 depends on the machine being idle. The owner's bar asks that one command takes a clean clone to all tests passing, and a UAE trading company's 100k-row lists depend on the same bulk path. Fix: give every bulk seed and COPY statement an explicit, generous timeout (or batch it), and run verify once under deliberate CPU contention (for example 3 parallel copies) before integrating. The same 3 tests are the reproduction.
- Gap evidence: verify.log lines with 'Failed Erp.Modules.Identity.Tests.UsersListVolumeTests.*' and 'System.TimeoutException : Timeout during reading attempt at IdentitySeeder.SeedVolumeAsync ... line 134'. verify-run2.log has the same 3 failures. volume-tests-rerun.log: the same tests pass alone at load 25. prior-attempt-at-1142805/up.log: ./erp up rc=1 on the same timeout. Reproduce: run 3 copies of ./erp verify at once on a 4-CPU machine, or run `dotnet test tests/Erp.Modules.Identity.Tests --filter UsersListVolumeTests` while the CPUs are saturated.

SELECT of 100,000 identity.user_credentials rows, and it runs under Npgsql's default 30 s command timeout. A second verify (verify-run2.log) failed the same 3 tests the same way. The suite stops after this failure, so the end-to-end stage never ran. Run alone on the host at a lower machine load (about 25), the same 3 tests passed in 85 s (volume-tests-rerun.log). - Biggest gap: **The G2 takeover gate never varies which field an edit changes, so the new e-mail correction can skip the access check unseen** — G2AccountTakeoverTests and GrantBearingRecords aim PUT /api/identity/users/{id} at the Administrator with one body only: the record's current values plus a changed displayName. Any path in Update that runs only when other fields change is never exercised against a stronger user. The e-mail correction is such a path, and it is new in this round. With a bypass that skips UserAccess.WithinCallerAsync when only the e-mail changes, every gate and identity test passes. Meanwhile a clerk holding users.update moves the Administrator's sign-in to an address of their choosing, and the Administrator is locked out: a takeover-class fault for the one account a UAE owner cannot lose.  The same blind spot exists on screen: no unit or e2e test opens Roles as a user without identity.roles.create, so a New role button shown to everyone passes (U1).  Fix: - For every non-GET user and role endpoint, build one request per writable property from the OpenAPI schema (email, displayName, displayNameAr, language, isActive, roleIds, permissions, nameEn, nameAr). Aim each at the Administrator, expecting 403 with the record unchanged, and a 2xx control on a user with no roles. - Add P5 as a gate self-test. - Extend the screens-hide matrix to every toolbar action: New user and New role for users who lack the create permission.

## 2026-10-03T23:01:52Z · p04-shell · round 3 · BLOCKED

- Commit judged: `dbd6e847d73ccfaab7982624eb212d648b9d42e3`
- Critic: fresh critic, p04-shell round 3
- Biggest gap: **The one-tab identity boundary is incomplete: B's searches and records reach tenant A through the tab's history and cookies, and no gate looks there** — In round 3 the shell forgets local, session and IndexedDB storage, Cache Storage and JS memory at sign-out, and the browser gate judges exactly those carriers. It does not cover the tab's own session history or cookies. The list and palette write the user's search text and the opened record's e-mail and id into the address (?q=, ?open=). location.replace('/') replaces only the current entry, so the earlier entries stay. When the next person in the same tab presses Back, their own screen opens with the previous tenant's search text and record. A cookie written by any screen also survives sign-out, and every gate passes. Picture a UAE bookkeeper serving several client companies from one browser, or a shared counter PC. The next client's user sees the previous client's supplier and customer names in their own search box. This also caps the shell's keyboard and Arabic wins over Odoo, because the same shared-device use is what the one-step language switch and keyboard paths serve. Fix: (1) Do not trust address state from history entries created by another identity: stamp history.state with the identity on pushState/replaceState, and drop q, open and similar parameters on load when the stamp differs or is missing. Or keep record ids and search text out of the URL for entries older than the session. (2) In forgetIdentity(), expire every non-HttpOnly cookie on the origin and reset window.name. (3) Add carriers to the browser gate: cookies (document.cookie and context.cookies()), window.name, and A pressing Back after signing in, judged on page, inputs and URL. Keep C2 and a history plant as gate self-tests.

## 2026-10-04T00:12:24Z · p01-odoo-rig · round 5 · BLOCKED

- Commit judged: `09d6d2c7dd1736a08aed8fa387cd47075a3af4f7`
- Critic: fresh critic, p01-odoo-rig round 5
- Biggest gap: **A driver can still act uncounted or off the clock from the harness's own Node process, so a gamed ours driver turns a loss into a recorded win** — Round 4 closed the browser side: fresh start context, page script refused, start state checked. The Node process the drivers run in is still open in three ways. (1) Network: installNetworkGuard patches only globalThis.fetch and http/https.request, and only on the first execute(). run.mjs loads every driver before that, so a fetch captured when the module loads is the raw one. process.getBuiltinModule('node:child_process' \| 'node:net' \| 'node:worker_threads') reaches the back end with no patch at all. The driver lint does not forbid getBuiltinModule or a captured fetch. (2) Timer: verify() runs after the clock stops, with the page's default timeout back at 120 s, and Locator.waitFor is allowed while verifying. A driver can return from run() right after its last click and let verify() wait for the end state for free. The README promises machine seconds run 'to the verified end state on screen', but nothing enforces it. (3) Paste: the PASTE regex misses Playwright's 'ControlOrMeta+v'. The copy-before-paste rule accepts any copy chord, even one with nothing selected, so set-up's clipboard is still pasted. Fixes: install the network guard before any driver loads. Forbid process.getBuiltinModule, process.binding and global captures of fetch in the lint, and refuse them at run time (wrap process.getBuiltinModule, or run drivers in a worker or vm with no process). Make verify() a single read with a short timeout, and require the end state to be on screen when run() returns: check it right at finish(), or let verify poll at most once. Normalise chords (ControlOrMeta, case, order) before matching paste. Require that the pasted text was selected and copied inside the measured part, for example by tracking the copied selection's text. Add each of my plants as a self-test.
- Gap evidence: Stand-in plants: copy gauntlet/evidence/p01-odoo-rig/r5/plants/zz-critic-r5.test.mjs into gauntlet/compare/test/ and run `node --test test/zz-critic-r5.test.mjs`. T2, U4, U5, C2 and C3 all end 'verified' (plants/critic-r5-plants.log): T2 0.107 s against the control's 2.175 s; U4 and U5 1 step with the back-end write done; C2 2 keys and C3 4 keys for 10 characters. Real driver: against `ERP_SEED_USERS_CSV=gauntlet/compare/data/out/users.csv ./erp up`, run `git apply plants/plant-U5-api-update-user-child-process.diff`, then `COMPARE_OURS_URL=http://localhost:<port> node run.mjs --task api-update-user --product ours`. It gives 'verified, steps 1, keys 56, machine 0.126 s, human 17.43 s', against Odoo's 2 / 184 / 0.733 / 54.62. An honest LOSS becomes a WIN (plants/real-driver-plants-run.log). The harness suite stays green with the plants: 146/146 (plants/npm-test-with-real-driver-plants.log). Script: plants/run-plants.sh.

## 2026-10-04T01:06:43Z · p00-foundation · round 4 · BLOCKED

- Commit judged: `bf32c911436237f8372050dc9d0839bdde1c8cc1`
- Critic: fresh critic, p00-foundation round 4
- Biggest gap: **The isolation gate never checks which tenant a request's SQL actually runs under, so a tenant switch inside the reviewed unit of work goes unseen unless the attack happens to send the right input** — The isolation gate has three layers: a source scan (it counts literal set_config/app.tenant_id uses per reviewed file), a SQL trace (it judges which class sends a setting statement) and a black-box attack (it sends tenant B values in inputs that were recorded or guessed). None of them compares the tenant actually bound, meaning the @tenant parameter of set_config or erp.current_tenant_id() after the statement, with the signed-in principal's tenant. So a rebind written inside ErpDbSession passes the source and trace layers by construction. It is caught only if the attack sends the triggering input by name. Here the input was a header read by enumeration, which RequestInputRecorder does not record, so all 88 gate tests and all 206 other .NET tests passed while tenant A could read tenant B's workspace and users. Round 2 asked for exactly this check: every statement inside an authenticated request must run with current_tenant_id() equal to the principal's tenant. It is still not enforced on values. Every later module (contacts, documents, imports, jobs) depends on this one guarantee. Suggested fixes: (1) In SqlTrace, judge every set_config('app.tenant_id', ...) by its parameter value against the request principal's tenant claim. Or append a check after each request statement, or read the bound tenant from the connection at commit. Fail on any mismatch. (2) Make RequestInputRecorder record header, query and cookie enumeration and treat any enumeration as 'reads every name'. Or send B's tenant id in arbitrary unknown header names. (3) Add T1d as a gate self-test plant.
- Gap evidence: In a clone of bf32c91, apply gauntlet/evidence/p00-foundation/r4/plant-T1d-support-rebind-enumerated.diff (ErpDbSession.cs and UnitOfWork.cs only). (a) Run dotnet test tests/Erp.Gates.Tests -c Release: 88/88 pass (plant-T1d.log). (b) Run ERP_PROJECT=x ERP_HTTP_PORT=20070 ERP_DB_PORT=20071 ERP_SEED_VOLUME=500 ./erp up, sign in as viewer@alnoor.example, and GET /api/tenancy/tenant and /api/identity/users with header 'Erp-Support-Workspace: 0190a000-0000-7000-8000-000000000002'. Both return 200 with gulfsteel's data (plant-T1d-live.txt). For contrast, plant-T1c-support-rebind.diff, the same rebind read with TryGetValue, is caught only by the canary scan: 5 leaks, while the trace judged its 98,396 setting statements as fine (plant-T1c.log).

Over HTTP (attack/http_attack.py, http_attack.out) I signed in as tenant A with cookie and bearer and sent 2,828 requests: 205 tenant B ids in every route, 8 tenant-switch headers, query and body fields. Result: 0 tenant B markers, and B's rows were unchanged. Targeted writes (attack/write_attack.out): PUT, DELETE, copy, password, unblock, revoke, access and sign-ins on B's user and role with valid bodies, and GET/PUT/DELETE on B's shared view. All returned 404, and B's records were unchanged afterwards. As erp_app in the database (attack/db-attack-app-role.txt): not superuser, no BYPASSRLS, member of nothing. With no tenant bound it reads 0 rows; a session-level set_config is ignored; bound to A it sees 0 B rows; UPDATE of B rows touches 0; an INSERT with B's tenant_id is refused by RLS; SET ROLE to erp_owner or erp_auth is denied; it cannot ALTER the table; it reads 0 credentials. verify_sign_in returns only an algorithm and salt challenge.   - G1 gate catches planted tenant leaks in the kernel (p00 surface): **FAIL** — T1d (plant-T1d-support-rebind-enumerated.diff) MISSED. The plant moves ErpDbSession's reviewed set_config command into a private ApplyAsync. After the session resolves, UnitOfWorkFilter calls ErpDbSession.SupportWorkspaceAsync, which loops over Request.Headers looking for 'Erp-Support-Workspace' and rebinds both the RLS tenant and the EF filter tenant to that value. I confirmed a real leak in a running stack built from the plant (plant-T1d-live.txt): tenant A's viewer, sending that header with gulfsteel's id, got 200 with gulfsteel's tenant record and its users. Yet the whole gate suite passed, 88/88 (plant-T1d.log), and so did Kernel, Identity, Lists and Tenancy, 206/206 (plant-T1d-other-suites.log). Why nothing caught it: the source gate counts set_config literals per file, and the refactor kept the count. The SQL trace judges only which class sends a setting statement, never which tenant it sets. The input recorder logs indexer, TryGetValue and ContainsKey reads but not enumeration, so the attack never tries that header. Non-interference and process-state checks do not look at binding. I did not run the G3 script (./erp verify --clean-clone) separately, because the run itself started from a clean clone. Times are warm-cache: the toolbox image and the NuGet/npm cache volumes already existed.

- Other findings:
  - Process-wide state outside fields gets through. Plant T2 kept a path-keyed answer cache in AppContext.SetData; the process-state inventory, which reflects over static and singleton fields only, did not flag it. The non-interference phase also saw 0 unstable comparisons, because its 'fresh process' is another test host in the same OS process and sees the same AppContext data. Suggest flagging AppContext.SetData, Environment.SetEnvironmentVariable and similar process-global stores in the source or process-state gate, and running the fresh comparison in a separate process.

## 2026-10-04T02:54:30Z · p03-identity · round 3 · BLOCKED

- Commit judged: `c02d93a8dd87c56fc1f06db51d95e6aa8ba761cb`
- Critic: fresh critic, p03-identity round 3
- Biggest gap: **The takeover and grant gates only aim at a record that grants everything, so a wrongly narrowed check that lets a clerk take over a non-identity manager goes unseen** — G2AccountTakeoverTests, GrantBearingRecords and the screens-hide matrix always use the strongest record as the target: the Administrator, or a role holding the whole catalogue. Every caller they build holds only identity permissions. A check that compares only part of the target's grants therefore still answers 403 against the Administrator, and every gate passes. Plants P14 and P16 make UserAccess and the role grant checks compare identity.* permissions only.  Result: a helpdesk clerk with users.update and resetPassword takes over the 'Workspace manager' (tenancy permissions). The clerk sets the manager's password, moves their sign-in, mints a role granting tenancy.tenant.update, and empties the manager's role. All 303 .NET tests and all 146 web tests stay green.  Once Contacts, Purchasing and Accounting land, most strong accounts in a UAE trading company will hold non-identity permissions: the finance manager, the purchasing approver. This class of fault is exactly the one that lets a junior administrator take them over.  Fix: - Aim every acting-on-a-user and acting-on-a-role probe at targets whose grants are disjoint from the caller's and only partly overlap them. Include one grant from every module in the catalogue, not only the Administrator, and include a test module the way ModulePermissionsTests does. - Add P14/P16 as gate self-tests. - Add a non-identity permission to the screens test catalogue and its stronger-user case (U2). - Separately, extend G1WriteOracle to name fields (L7): a role created by B, then A's same-name create, must answer like an unused name.
- Gap evidence: In a clone of c02d93a: 1. git apply gauntlet/evidence/p03-identity/r3/plants/P14-P16.diff 2. dotnet test Erp.slnx -c Release gives Gates 93/93, Identity 74/74, Kernel 127/127, Lists 6/6, Tenancy 3/3 (plants/plant-P14-P16-all-dotnet.log). 3. Copy plants/CriticPartialGrantProof.cs to tests/Erp.Modules.Identity.Tests/ and run with --filter CriticPartialGrantProof. The answers are: reset password 200, move sign-in 200, grant the helper the manager role 403, create a role with tenancy.tenant.update 201, rename and empty the manager role 200 (plants/plant-P14-P16-proof.log). Without the plant all five answer 403 (plants/proofs-unplanted-control.log). 4. UI: git apply plants/U2-ui-beyond-own-identity-only.diff, then in web run npx vitest run: 146/146 (plants/plant-U2-web-unit.log). 5. Leak: python3 plants/apply-L7.py, then dotnet test tests/Erp.Gates.Tests gives 93/93 (plants/plant-L7-gates.log). CriticRoleNameOracleProof.cs shows 409 for B's role name and 201 for an unused one (plants/plant-L7-proof.log).
- Hard gates:
  - G1 tenant isolation of the product as built: **PASS** — My own attacks found no leak. As alnoor's admin I made 24 API calls against gulfsteel's admin, a second B user, the B Administrator role and the B Read-only role. They covered GET, PUT (including an e-mail move), password, unblock, revoke, DELETE, role PUT/DELETE/copy, create-with-B-role, search and filter by B values, a tenant query parameter and tenant headers. Every call by id answered 404, searches returned nothing of B's, B's records read back unchanged, and B's admin still signed in. I also hit B's private and shared saved views 12 ways: all 404 (attacks/attack-a-vs-b.txt). As erp_app: 0 rows with no tenant bound. Bound to A, 0 of B's rows and UPDATE 0. An insert into B was refused by RLS. A session-level or forged app.tenant_tx tenant was ignored. Credentials and sign_in_secret were denied, SET ROLE was refused, and RLS could not be disabled (attacks/db-app-role.txt). No export, job or file endpoints exist yet.
  - G1 gate catches a tenant leak planted in this piece's surface: **FAIL** — Plant L7 (plants/L7-role-name-registry.diff) keeps a role-name registry outside the tenant's rows, so POST /api/identity/roles and /roles/{id}/copy answer 409 identity.roleNameTaken for a name another workspace uses. With it in place all 93 gate tests pass (plants/plant-L7-gates.log) and so do all 74 identity tests (plant-L7-identity-tests.log). Proof (plants/CriticRoleNameOracleProof.cs, plant-L7-proof.log): tenant B creates 'JAFZA payroll …', then tenant A gets 409 for that name and 201 for an unused one. Without the plant both answer 201 (proofs-unplanted-control.log). G1WriteOracle judges only e-mail and code fields, so name fields are never checked for write oracles.
